( ii ) तारीख 8 जुलाई. 1989 में प्रकाणिम भाग्म परकार के मंचार मंत्रालय ( डाक विभाग ) की अधिसूचना मं . का . प्रा . 1587 तारीरर 7 जन , 1989 को

1. इस प्रादेश की अनुसूची के भाग । के स्तम्भ 1 में विनिर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सेवा सम् रिस में पदों के संबंध में मतम्भ ? में विनिर्मिष्ट अधिकारी नियुक्नि 
प्राधिकारी होगा और स्तम्भ 3 में विनिदिष्ट प्राधिकारी स्तम्भ 1 में विनिर्दिष्ट शास्तियों की बाबम अन गाममिक प्राधिकारी होगा : 

2 . उक्त अनुसूची के भाग 2 के मम्भ 1 में विनिर्दिष्ट साधारण केन्द्रीय सेवा समाह ग और ममह घ में पक्षों के मन में निम्न में विनिविष्ट 
प्राधिकारी नियुक्ति प्राधिकारी लोगा और स्तम्भ 3 नया 5 में विनिर्दिष्ट प्राधिकारी सम्म 4 में विनिदिष्ट शास्तियों की बाबत श्रमण : प्रनशासनिक प्राधिकारी

का . मा . 2459. -- कर्मचारी भविष्य निधि और प्रकीगं 
उपबंध अधिनियम 1952 ( 1952 का 19 ) की धारा 5क , 
की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए 
केन्द्रीय सरकार श्री लान बहादुर सिंह के स्थान पर श्री समर 
चक्रवर्ती को केन्द्रीय न्यासी बोर्ड का सस्दय नियुक्त करती है 

और भारत के असाधारण राजपन्न भाग- II , खंड 3 उपखंड 
( ii ) दिनांक 18 सितम्बर , 1985 में प्रकाशित भारत 
सरकार के श्रम मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना संख्या का . प्रा . 677 
( ई ) दिनांक 18 सितम्बर , 1985 में निम्नलिखित संशोधन 
करती है : - - -

12 . The fact that Gainwad had appeared for the Workman 
on the last 3 dittos, does not, in the peculiar circumstances 
of the presenti case , make any difference . Gaikwad could 
do nothing for the Workman . On the last date , the Enquiry 
Ollicer scored the unusual course of exhibiting documents, 
one after the other , without examining any witness connected 
with ! ! 1 . The ser ousness of the questions involved was 
not suifficiently precent to his mind . He should have granted 
the Workman s request for Lawyer, although it had techni 
cally coine late . On the peculiar facts of the case , I am 
impressed by the Management s submission that Caikwad s 
acci tairce should be treated. as adequate . 

13. The result of the above discussion is that in the present 
case serious prejudice has resulted to the Workman by the 
Enquiry Officer s declining the request of the Workman to 
engage a lawyer. In the peculiar and special circumstances of 
the case , the Workman was very much entitled to a Lawyer s 
assistance . Indeed the prejudice caused is so gross that in 
the words used in Furtado s case , there has been a failure 
of the inquiry itself . Findings on serious charges have been 
reached in absence of legal evidence . Indeed I would have 
heen inclined to treat the inquiry as had , even de hors the 
factor of lawyer s assistance. 

14 . The domestic inquiry is held to be had in law . As 
the Manarement have reserved their option in their written 
statement to lead evidence on the merits of the charges , hoth 
-ides will be entitled to lead evidence before the Trib ;inal. 
Costs so far to abide the final result .

का . आ . 2463: - - औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम 
1947 ( 1947 का 14 ) की धारा 17 के अनमरण में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार बिडला सीमेंट वर्कम, चनापत्थर खान , 
चन्देरिया चित्तौड़गढ़ ( राजस्थान ) के प्रबन्धतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों 

और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच , अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक 
विवाद में औद्योगिक अधिकरण , उदयपर के पंचाट को 
प्रकाशित करती है , जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 24- 8- 90 को 
प्राप्त हुया था ।

नलतिः इसके 7 में विपक्षी पानिमिति मार्ग 
कि बम्पर में कोई तकनीकी कामो ना यो गति का 
में प्रार्थी लापरवाहीपूर्ण तरीके से डम्पर को चला रहा था 
कि जिससे यह घटना घटित हुई जिससे घटनास्थल पर ही 
दुर्घटनाग्रस्त श्रमिक की मृत्य हो गई । उन्होंने रह भी नई 
दिया कि जबाव दावे में लिख्ने अनुमार पहले भी पार्थी को 
कई लापरवाहियों के बाबत चेतावनी दी गई अत: ऐसे प्रार्थी 
के साथ किमी प्रकार की कोई रियायन नहीं की जानी 
चाहिए । 

दोनों ओर से प्रस्तुत तर्क वितर्क के संदर्भ में इस प्रकरण 
की भभी परिस्थितियों पर विचार करने के उपरान्त 
यह देखना है कि इस प्रकरण में किम प्राशय का एवाई 
पारित किया जाना चाहिये । 

सभी परिस्थितियों पर विचार करने के उपरान्त मेरी 
राय में प्रार्थी पर ऐसी गंभीर दुर्घटना पूर्व में कारित 
करने का आरोप नहीं है बल्कि इस तरह का यह आरोप 
पहला ही प्रतीत होता है और किसी भी परिस्थितियों में 
वाहन चालक द्वारा कभी भी कोई दुर्घटना हो सकती है 
लेकिन जब फौजदारी न्यायालय में नक संदेह ने परे यह 
तथ्य प्रमाणित नही होता कि वास्तव में प्रार्थी की जानबूझकर , 
लापरवाही व गफलत थी तब तक सेवामुक्ति जैसा कठोर 
दंड देना मेरी राय में उचित नही है और प्रार्थी को अपने 
आप में सुधार करने के रूप में एक अंतिम अवसर विपक्षी 
के यहां सेवा में कार्यरत रहने का एक अवसर मौजूदा समय 
को भयंकर मंहगाई व बेरोजगारी को देखते हए दिया जाना 
चाहिये । 

मैने इस संदर्भ में समझौता अधिकारी की रिपोर्ट का 
अवलोकन किया कि जिसमें समझौता अधिकारी द्वारा विपक्षी 
को यह सुझाव दिया गया था कि प्रार्थी को नौकरी से निकालने 
के बजाय पदावनति का दंड दिया जाये और प्रार्थी को सबसे 
नीची श्रेणी पर नियुक्ति दे दी जाय लेकिन विपक्षी प्रबन्धक 
राजी नहीं हुये क्योंकि प्रार्थी का पिछला रेकार्ड खराब है । 
मेरी राय में विपक्षी को समझौता अधिकारी का यह 
सुझाव मान लेना चाहिये था क्योंकि मेरी राय में उक्त 
सुझाव इस प्रकरण की परिस्थिति के संदर्भ में न्यायोचित 
प्रतीत होता है । अतः धारा 11 ( ए ) में प्रदन शक्तियों का 
उपयोग करते हुए प्रार्थी को मेवामक्ति से कम दंड दिया 
जाना न्यायोचित प्रतीत होता है । 

अतः इस प्रकरण में इस आशय का एवार्ड पारित किया 
जाता है कि बिड़ला सिमेन्ट वन चना पन्थर खान, चन्देरिया , 
चितौड़गढ़ द्वारा डम्पर ड्राईवर श्री जग्गु को 10- 12- 87 
मे मेवा से पृथक करना न्यायोचित नहीं है और इसमें भाग 
11 ( ए ) के प्रावधान के अनुसार न्यायोचित कमी करना

चालक के पद से एक पद नीचे के पद पर पदस्थापित किया

1946 , till such time the said Writ Petition is dispused of .

it is still pending before the Delhi High Court . This fact 
is not denied by the Union . Therefor . I find that the

Corporation Act , 1953 , will prevail oir the Model Standing

levelled against him . It is further seen that a copy of the 
Repoil dated 18- 6 - 1984 was sent to the workman along 
wili ile chargesheet. He was further told that in case he 
wanted to put in written statement in reply to the charges 
he should submit it within seven days, failing which it would 
be pre - uiled that he did not want to put in any written 
statement. Ex . 9 are the copies of the enquiry proceedings . 
It is seen therefrom that the workman Shri U . D . Yadav 
had perficinaied in the proceedings . It is seen from those 
enquiry proceedings that he admitted before the Enquiry 
Officer that he understood the charges framed against him 
and they were very clear to him . It is seen that two wit 
nesses were examined on behalf of the management, namely , 
Shri U Rama Rao , Office Superintendent, and Shri 0 . P . 
Binahu , Chief Plant Officer . Both these witnesses were 
cross -examined by the workman himself . The workman also 
examined one witness on his behalf , namely Shri P . K . 
War . He was also cross- examined on behalf of the manage 
mert. After the recording of evidence was over, the work 
man filed his submissions in writing before the Enquiry Offi 
cer . Thereafter , the Enquiry Officer submitted his report in 
the matter . I. therfore , find that the chargesheet issued 
against the workman and the enquiry proceedings held 
against him were quite as per law and were not bad in law . 
Tesue No. 1 is therefore found in the negative . AC 
cording to the Union , the findings of the Enquiry 
Officer are serverse . The said witnesses for the 
management deposed in support of the case of the manage 
ment. The witness Shri Wagh examined on behalf of the 
workman deposed in support of the case of the workman 
namely , that the workman had attended his duty on 18th . 
19th and 20th June , 1984 and that he had not participated 
in the illegal strike , nor had he done any illegal acts on 
any of these threc days . As per the evidence of the manage 
ment witness Shri Rama Rao before the Enquiry Officer , 
thic workman had attended the duty on the 18th instant, 
but on 19th instant he was marked absent and had not 
worked on that dav . and that he had also not reported for 
duty on the 20th instant. As per the evidence of the other 
mangement witness Shri Bimrahw , on 18 - 6 - 1984 the work 
mom was among non -working helners . As per the evidence 
of the workman s witness Shri Wagh , he had allotted the 
duty to the workman from 18 - 6 - 84 to 20 - 6 - 1984 , and that 
the workman har attended his duty accordingly . 

8 . A rany of the Enguiry renort is at Ex . 13 . After 
diccuceiro the evidence of the two management witnesses 
an , the witness for the workman , the Fnauiry Officer con 
cluded thus 
“ While going through the proceedings it will be ob 

served that Shri Yadav was present on 18th and 
19th June , 1984 . However , he deserted the place 
of work on 19 June and did not work along with 
others and thereby resorted to illegal strike. On 
18th June, 1984 he also participated in the mob , 
who tried to prevent the Technicians from re 
pairing the G . S . Equipment and also shouted and 
gesticulated in such a manner that violence was 
imminent. 

In view of the above , I hold Shri Yadav guilty 
of the charges under Clause 6 ( c ) and sub - clauses 
9 , 18 , 19 and 2 of Clause 28 of the Standing 
Orders applicable to him and also interference 
with the work of other employees . " 


Thus, the name of Shri U . D . Yadav is appearing in this 
complaint among other persons who had taken part in the 
illegal acts committed on that day . There was no reason 
Why Shri Bimrahw should have lodged a false complaint, as 
ahove, against the said workmen on the very day of that 
incident. Therefore, taking into consideration this coniplaint 
and the evidence of the two management witnesses and the 
statements made by the workman s witness Shri Wagh in his 
cross -examination , was the conclusions arrived at by the 
Enquiry Officer could not be termed as perverse . I find that 
the findings of the Enquiry Officer are just and proper . Issue 
No. 2 is therefore found in the negative . 
ISSUFS NOS. 4 and 5 

9 . As such the action of the management of Indian Airlines 
in relation to its establishment at Bombay in imposing the 
punishment of stoppage of one increment with effect from 
1 - 8 - 1985 on Shri U . D . Yadav , Helper is just and proper . 
Issue No. 4 is found in the affirmative . As such , the worman 
concerned is not entitled to any relief. Issue No. 5 is found 
accordingly . 


ISSUES NOS. 6 and 7 


10 . It is not disputed that the management had paid 50 % 
of the wages to the worman of the period of his suspension . 
According to the Union , as the suspension was withdrawn , 
the worman was entitled to full wages as per the provisions 
of the model Standing Orders framed under the Industrial 
Employment ( Standing Orders ) Act , 1946 . Howevei, as no 
tcd arove , the employees of the said inanagement are gover 
ned by the Standing Orders framed under the Air Corporation 
Act, 1953 , and not by the Standing Orders framed under 
the Industrial Employment ( Standing Orders ) Act 1946 . 
According to the management as per the provisions of the 
standing orders as they apply to the said workman , he is not 
untitled to full wages of the suspension period, but is entitled 
to only 1 . 2 of the wages. I find that the said contention 
of the management is quite tenable in law . Therefore , the 
allegation of Air Corporation Employees Union , Bombay , 
that the management of Indian Airlines, Bombay , have not 
made due payment of subsistence allowance and other dues 
frcal 20 - 6 -1984 to 6 - 7 - 1984 to Shri U . D . Yadav, Helder is 
not correct. Issue No . 6 is therefore found in the negative . 
As such the workman is not entitled to any relef. Issue No 
1 is found accordingly .

upon him , was issued to him . However , the punishemt 
\proposed to be imposed was only a minor one , and as such 
no injustice has been caused to the workman by not issuing 
show cause notice to him .

FINDINGS WITH REASONS 

10 . Issues No . 1 to 3 : - - ) will take up all these 
issues together . It ii true that it has been challen 
ged that the workman and the burden lies on the 
workman concerned to prove that he is a worknian . 
Without going into the details of those facts which 
were not hotly contested It can ve safely said that 
the workman was workman within the definition of 
Sec . 2 (s ) of the I. D . Act . These facts can be well 
gathered from the docunients Ex. M1 and Ex. M2, 
his attendance register [ x . M |3 to Ex . M 29 as also 
Ex. M ! 30 with annexure list of the candidates . Ex . 
M31 is Agreement between the management and the 
employees Federation , accordin . to which the work 
men who had completel one year service should be 
absorbed . Ex. M32 is another letter. 

11. Management has examined R . L . Agarwal 
Officer of the Bank in support of its case while the 
workman examined none. 

12 . According to this witness the workman had 
joined at Seoni Branch , State Bank of India , as tem 
porary Cashier- cum -Clerk on 15 - 11 - 1965 . He was 
appointed against leave vacancy of S . K . Jain and 
was given an appointment order. He brought the 
register for the per od from 15 - 11-65 to 31- 12 - 66 . 
Accoding to him , workman worked upto 31 - 12 - 66 
and his services were terminaied with effect from 
1 - 1 - 1967 . 

13 . Taking the case as it is , it is obvious that the 
management has not counted his service prior to thic 
period 1 - 1- 1966 . According to the workman , he un 
disputedly served from 15 - 11 - 1965 and this fact has 
been admitted by the management. 

14 . As I have already pointed out above, manage 
ment has deniia that the workman concerned had . 
worked from & - 11 - 1965 to 31- 12 - 1965 i.e . for a 
period of 54 days it follo :vs that the management has 
nothing to say against it . In its various stateinents 
and rejoinders they have given details of dates during 
which this workman had worked in the year 1966 . 
Even M . W . 1, R . L . Agarwal, has partly admitted 
the case of the workman inasmuch as he admitted 
that this workma! joined the State Bank of India on 
15 - 11 - 1965 . The management has, however, filed 
the attendance register of the workman since 
20 - 12 - 1966 onwarris and not from 8 - 11 - 1965 Thus 
there is nothing to disbelieve this part of the version 
of the workman that he had workea from 8 - 11 - 1965 
to 31 - 12 - 1965 in the ycar 1965. It follows that he 
worked for 54 davs upto 31- 12 - 1965 . Thus if this 
period is added to the perior of working days of this 
workman as given by the management i.e . 198 days, 
it would exceerd 249 days . But the question is whe 
ther this period of 54 days should also be computed 
for the purposes of computing under the provisions 
of Sec . 25B read with Sec . 25F of the I. D . Act, for 
this period of service of the workman has been com 
pletely ignored by the managenient and according to 
the management the year commences from 1st day 
of each English Calendar month and ends with last 
last day of the English Calendar month .

31- 12 - 1966 . The l orkman was provided witl : an 
opportunity to appear in the recruimient test but lic 
failed to qualify the same, hence period for service 
as claimed by the workman was denied . Once the 
opportunity having been given to the workmen and 
because he failed to qualify the same it rendered hiin 
disentitle to appear in subsequent tests . Reference 
is , therefore, liable to be dismissed .

down that the service shal. bc decmed to be contin 
uous inspite of its interruption on account of any 
of the reasons added therein and should remain un 
interrupted . While dealing with Sub -section ( 1 ) re 
lated to continuous survice for a period , it consists 
of two parts ie , (i) uninterrupted service — the use of 
thc words " svall be said to be in continuous service " 
makes the part of the definition exhaustive i. e . all in 
terrupted service means continuous service , on ac 
count of the following reasons viz . (a ) sickness (6 ) 
unauthorised leave (c ) accident (d ) strike wilich 18 
not illegal ( e ) lock out and (f ) cessation of work i. c . 
not due to any fault on the part of the workman shall 
be included in the continuous service . 

20 . Thus by fiction the legislaturc has included 
service which may be interrupted on account of any 
of the reasons in " continuous service for the pur 
pose of this Chapter. These interruptions have to 
be ignored to treat the workman ini uninterrupted 
service and such service uninterrupted on account 
of these cases which would be deemed to be uninter 
rupted would be " continuous service " for the period 
for which the workman had been in service . ( See 
also Kishore B . Dave Vs. The Dean , J . J . Group of 
Hospitals and oth :: 3 ( FLR 1986 (52 ) p . 330 ) paras 
12 & 13 ). 

21. In the case of N . Sunderamoney referred to 
above , the workman was appointed off and on by tlic 
State Bank of India between July 31 , 1973 and 
August 29 , 1973, it was observed that the interrait 
tent breaks notwithstanding his total number of days 
of employment answered the test of “ deemed " con 
tinuous service within Sec. 25B (2 ) and both the side 
had accepted this situation ( ara 5 of the judgment ). 

22. In the instant case, cessation of the work was 
not due to any fault on the part of the workman 
and therefore it shall be included in the continuous 
service . Though the workinan has specifically plead 
ed in para 13(vii) of his statement that he was ready 
and willing to serve but he was not given work and 
this fact has not been controverted by the manage 
ment, it therofors cannot be said that cessation of 
work was due to any fault on the part of the work 
man and as such he would be deemed to be in con 
tinuous service despite the interruption in the service 
as pointed out above . While explaining words 
" actually worked " used in Sec . 25B ( A ) I would like 
to refer the case o ? Workizen of American Express 
International Banking Corporation Vs. Management 
of American Express International Banking Corpo 
ration ( 1985 FIR (51) p . 483 ) . The relevant part 
of para 5 of the judgment runs as thus : 
" The expression which we are required to 

construc is actually worked under the 
employer . This expression , according to 
us, cannot mean those days only when the 
workian worked with hammer. sickle or 
pen , but must necessarily comprehend all 
those days during which he was in the 
employnnt of the employer and for which 
he had by un paid wages wither under ex 
press or itslied contract of service or by

14 . With regard to Kamlesh Montessory School, 
the E . O , has observed as follows at page 3 of the 
inquiry report , 
In his cross examination Shri Sareen has admit 

ted that Kamlesh Montessory School is 
functioning in the house acquired by Shri 
Sareen with the aid of Housing Loan from 
the bank since 1985 and he did not seek 
any permission from the bank in this regard 
or gave any notice to the bank . . . . . . . . 
I exoperate Shri Sareen of the charge that 
he is being paid some monthly rent by the 
said Nursery School, however, Sh . Sareen 
in his cross examination has stated that his 
is a joint family comprising of his mother , 
brother, unmarried sister, wife and children 
and they are living in the same house . His 
living in a joint family alongwith his mother 
and brother who are running the nursery 
school lie must be the beneficiary from the 
income of the school indirectly , if not direc 
ly . . . . Shri Shanti Ranjan M . W . 1 in his 
deposition has stated that the school has 
more than 100 children and 3 / 4 tcachers 
are working there . Students are charged 
fee and other dues. The defence did not 
cross examine the M . W . 1, on this issue 
and as such it is established that Shri Sareen 
is using the house acquired by him with 
the help of housing loan for commercial 
purposes in violation of Housing Loan Ru 
les of the bank . 


has not named his brother in the said application . 
Thuy are dependant on him or all of them are mem 
bers of a joint family is not of nuch importance , It 
is crystal clear that a portion of the house purchased 
by Shri Sareen with the help of loan sanctioned to 
him by the bank is being used for commercial pur 
poses . The number of students is by no means sliall . 
Therefore, this charge fully stands p . oved. 

15 . Thus the findings given by the E .O . with regard 
to charge Nos. 1 and 2 are not at all perverse , they are 
based on legal evidence . Point No 1 is decided uc 
cordingly . 
POINT NO . 2 

16 . Looking to the nature of the charges , the 
punishment awarded cannot be called as dispropor 
tionate or excessive . From the rules governing the 
grant of housing loans it appears that the object of 
the scheme is 10 asyist an employee who does not 
own any residential house . It further appears that 
the loan for house granted at a comparatively low 
interest . The rates of interest are given at page 3 of 
the Rules . Interest at commercial rate is certainly 
much higher . The purpose will stand defeated if in 
tho garb of acquiring the residential house with the 
help of housing loan an employec tries to run a trade 
or business in ir either himself or through one or 
some of the members of his family . On point No . 2 
also the Union workman files . 

17 . Hence , it is held that the punishment of 
stoppage of iwo annual increments with cumulative 
effect having the effect of postponing his future in 
crements awarded by the management of Union Book 
of Indian cannot be held in violation of the principles 
of natural justice , Conscquently , the workinan is held 
entitled to no 1clicf.

Shri Mishra that the statements attributed to Sh . 
Sareen by the E . O , are incorrect nor he argued that 
the statements of M . W - 1 Sh . Shanti Ranjan referred 
to , by the E . O , arc incorrect. Therefore , on the basis 
of evidence referred to by the E . O , his finding cannot 
be called hy any stretch of imagination as not based on 
evidence, it is neither incorrect nor perverse . We have 
seen that in his application for loan he has shown his 
unmarried sister and mother as dependant on him . He
